member of the commission. – madam president, i am very proud to stand in front of this assembly for the first time as the commissioner for economy. i look forward to the important work ahead of us, and the fight against tax fraud is high on our respective agendas. i would like to thank the rapporteur, lídia pereira, and the members of committee on economic and monetary affairs (econ) for their excellent work on the two proposals we are discussing. vat fraud is a major issue affecting all member states and citizens alike, and it is important that measures aimed at attacking vat fraud can be subject to a democratic debate in this plenary. the present proposal should be examined in line with the vat and e—commerce package that was adopted by the council in december 2017, and will enter into application in january 2021. the proposal provides tax authorities with a powerful tool that will allow them to detect and fight vat fraud in the e-commerce sector. european action is absolutely necessary. in 2015, as the rapporteur remembers, eur 5 billion of vat was lost in the european union solely on imports of goods. similar numbers have been reported with regard to supplies of services. this problem is also likely to keep growing in future, considering the growth rate of e-commerce in europe. the commission has proposed to create an obligation for payment service providers established in the union to transmit data on the beneficiary of cross-border payments they execute. this obligation was designed to limit administrative burdens in that regard. the requested information is already available and used by payment service providers to process transactions. although this proposal constitutes a big step forward in the fight against vat fraud, there is still much to do. this year’s commission study on the vat gap shows that the member states were in general losing eur 137 billion in 2017, and these numbers are obviously still too high. because of this, i intend to make the fight against tax fraud a top priority of my mandate. of course the fight against tax fraud goes hand in hand with improving tax compliance. this is why these two aspects will be part of a new action plan i will present next year. eu business must be able to access the eu market without constraints and unfair competition by those that are not compliant. eu citizens must be able to legally access the worldwide market and benefit from the redistribution of the revenue collected.
i am speaking on behalf of the ppe group. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. we are again faced each year with a vat gap of eur 137.5 billion, or 11.2% of expected vat revenues, with cross-border vat fraud of over eur 50 billion and with growing tax fraud in online commerce, where over 90% of transactions are handled through payment service providers. i am therefore very pleased, firstly, commissioner, with your announcements about the action plan, but also with the two reports by my colleague mrs pereira, because with both reports we are taking a very important step towards creating a single european vat area and these measures complement the new vat framework for online trade, which is due to come into force in january 2021. we urgently need a system of common rules instead of a system of exceptions and loopholes. we must put an end to vat fraud and we must stop the unacceptable theft of taxpayers' money. i therefore say to the council, despite the positive decisions of 8 november: whoever blocks more european and common european tax policy is supporting the fraudsters and supporting waste. we must therefore fight this instrument of unanimity. we need more cooperation between tax authorities and payment service providers, harmonised requirements for payment service providers for cross-border payments, access to information by anti-fraud agencies and a new central electronic system for storing payment information.
on behalf of the s&d group. madam president, i have to ask you to leave. one of the european union's most important tasks is social justice, ensuring a life worthy of every citizen of the union. and a basic condition for that is fair taxation. the fight against crossborder tax evasion is therefore one of the most important tasks of the european union, and of us as members of parliament. we cannot continue to allow a whole series of multinationals to avoid taxation across europe because of loopholes, lack of cooperation between member states or the cynicism and cynical oversight of a few member states. as a hungarian representative, i come from a country with the highest vat rate in the european union and indeed in the world, at 27%. it also means that my country is one of the tax cheats' favorite target areas, hunting grounds. as a hungarian, i know exactly what it's like to have public money go to crooks. tax evasion is not the brainchild of a few international con artists, as it causes 137 billion euros of damage across europe. tax fraud is therefore a crime that seriously threatens the functioning of education, health, social services and public services in general, undermines social justice, our common europe without borders, and of course destroys european democracy and endangers the peace of everyday life. it is therefore very important that we have been able to draw up jointly the draft measures on crossborder vat fraud and to put them on the table in parliament. as we promised, we've worked with you to make sure that these taxes are not just paid by those who work for a living, by family businesses, but that no one cheats, no one exploits their privilege. these proposals will strengthen cooperation and exchange of information between member states, ensure long-term data capture and thus ensure effective action against cross-border tax fraud. so i ask you to vote yes to this, each and every one of these proposals deserves to become law.

madam president, i would like to speak on behalf of the pirate delegation. first, it needs to be mentioned that we consider vat fraud an important topic that needs to be addressed. in this regard, we are grateful that it is being worked on. however, we can’t support the way the issue is being handled. the whole concept of retaining huge amounts of data without any clear selection of what is really needed and what is thought unnecessary is problematic. at this moment, just 25 cross—border payments quarterly, including offline payments by credit card, are enough to store payees’ data, including credit card numbers, for up to five years. this will create huge amounts of data that will flow with little restriction through databases, seriously lowering privacy and incurring infrastructure costs for such databases, meaning that this whole solution is completely unproportionate to real vat fraud checks. yes, we need to use modern technologies to help fight tax fraud. however, in this case we are using a needlessly massive tool that is well capable of causing much more harm than good.
i'm just repeating what i said in english to the previous report, because they're deeply connected. i consider the fight against vat evasion to be very important, as do most other pirates. but at the same time, we think that the solution we're proposing is completely unsuitable for the problem we're facing. with this solution, we would store all payments made by a payer who makes more than 25 payments in a three-month period, which we consider to be a completely inadequate invasion of privacy. at the same time, the data thus retained will flow across europe with very few restrictions, risking the leakage of this highly sensitive data that tells us so much about our citizens. in view of this, we have decided to abstain and consider it appropriate given the complex nature of the matter we are discussing here.

madam chairman, we have a record. nine czechs are speaking today on one specific point. half of the national deputies. why would you do that? it's about money, nothing less, nothing more. the czechs are afraid of being shamed into wearing a coat. on the one hand, the czech government is headed by a prime minister and a businessman who draws and consumes. on the other side is the european commission, which logically blames prime minister babiš for a conflict of interest. the first argues with czech laws, the second with european legislation. we need to get this cleared up, and we need to do it fast. it's going to be too late. next year, a new budget is being negotiated. normally i say that subsidies are like the plague, the disease spreads fast. but we can't do the green revolution without money. i therefore ask the european commission to resolve this particular conflict of interest issue quickly and transparently. i've already interviewed you, but we're still waiting for a response.
i think it is very good, madam president, that we are talking here in the plenary about the financial interests of the european union, and specifically about the money of all europeans. this issue, as i said, is currently connected to perhaps the biggest conflict of interest that we have ever dealt with in the european union, and that is unfortunately the conflict of interest of czech prime minister andrej babiš. i am very pleased, commissioner, that after some hesitation, the european commission's experts have finally carried out an audit and that you have therefore finally issued a decision. i think it's important that this decision be transparent, that it be credible and that people believe the arguments you're making in it. because andrej babiš controls the media, has enormous media influence and of course presents the whole thing as evil brussels officials attacking the brave andrej babiš. that's why it's bad that the final report isn't public, even though it's final. please publish this report, take steps to increase transparency so that the citizens of the european union know the official arguments and believe that andrej babiš is illegally draining european subsidies and thus their money. hundreds of thousands of people are demonstrating in the czech republic and wanting clear answers from europe.
madam president, first of all i am glad that the president of the cont committee has come here with a clarification question and i am glad that, in her reply to one of my colleagues, she has pointed out that in fact 95-96% of the funds are correct, because all european citizens are listening to us here and i would not like to see all european funds being wasted and there is corruption with all european funds. but, commissioner, i appreciate your honesty. you said you needed to document yourself to answer some of the questions, the ones our colleague reported today. i think there are solutions. conflict of interest is not defined equally in all member states as we speak, and imbalances are emerging. then we have to prevent, as has been said before, and i think the things that have been highlighted here, namely: money, for example, for farmers. for years, and in my country, i've seen that uncultivated land is also given. you have to give it to the people who work the land and not to the people who have accumulated and kept the land and exploited the others, after all. then you have to make an anti-fraud strategy. of course we have an anti-fraud strategy, but you have to improve the mechanisms so that, year by year, we reduce fraud and corruption on european funds and the responsibility is shared at the level of the member states as well.
madam president, i regret seeing that some colleagues are using this debate to generate national—oriented political attacks instead of having a serious and constructive discussion. for this i would advise them to go to their national parliament. however, let me contribute to the discussion in a factual manner, as the shadow rapporteur for the common provision regulation, also called cpr, and outline some planned future improvements in the use of the new fund. cpr aims to establish a single rule book for the period 21-27, including enabling conditions, a key element for the control of member states’ fund spending. programmes funded by eu resources will have to respect enabling conditions and preconditions in order to achieve effective implementation. if conditions are not fulfilled, member states will not be able to claim for the payment of their expenditures. on top of that, besides enabling conditions, cpr new rules require to measure the performance of the funds. for example, the member states will have to conduct a mid—term review. so there are tools.
mr president, the disability strategy 2020 is coming to an end, so let us now try to create a new, more ambitious one that can implement all the objectives of the convention on the rights of persons with disabilities. this convention has been signed and ratified by all the member states, which is a big commitment. the commissioner said that disabled people should be treated as fully equal, and that we should take special account of the rights of women and children with disabilities. the accessibility of buildings, including offices, cultural and other institutions, remains a major problem. speaking of accessibility of buildings, it is appropriate to mention the facilitation of movement for people with disabilities. it would also be appropriate to pursue the uniform treatment of the discount on motorway charges for disabled persons within the schengen area, the barrier-free use of means of transport in public transport or a uniform disability card. by educating and empowering people with disabilities, we will make a significant contribution to reducing their... (the president withdrew the floor.)
mr president, commissioner, i congratulate you on your responsible role. population and species diversity are essential for agricultural production, for our food security and for the improvement of biodiversity. farming properly can make a significant contribution to these issues. as you know, we are currently carrying out a reform of the agricultural policy, and that is a great opportunity, not a risk, as we hear so often here. but it is essential that we find enough funding in the cap and that we include the investments of our farmers in these areas in the category of climate and biodiversity expenditure. investment is also needed in the development, alternatives and deployment of new technologies. i trust that you will support your colleague wojciechowski in these areas in the months ahead.
mr. chairman, i often warn against rash, ideologically motivated actions and i warn that these actions will have consequences. ten years ago, this parliament approved the promotion of biofuels in the name of green ideology. the result is a thousand yellow ribbons everywhere you look. the czech prime minister takes the subsidies and then he makes a profit from it, a conflict of interest, we talked about it yesterday. the fields are full of herbicides and pesticides, roundup they call it. the victims? millions of bees and other pollinators are dying, tragedy. that is why i was happy to support today's initiative, but we should not forget that the european parliament also paved the way for this tragedy ten years ago.
madam president, i would like to say to mr borrell that we are talking about the recent leaks, the recent leaks that reveal that chinese ambassadors and high-ranking government officials have been straight-up lying to us over the mass cultural genocide that china commits. yesterday, i was fortunate enough to meet with mr ilham tohti’s daughter, jewher, but it could not have been a meeting with mr ilham tohti himself because he has been locked up in a concentration camp and we haven’t heard about his whereabouts or wellbeing for two years. jewher doesn’t oppose china, i don’t oppose china. she just wants her family to be safe and together. at the same time as this genocide is taking place, in my country the richest man, petr kellner, has been manipulating the czech media image of china to look more business positive – business! excuse me, but if i made this speech in china, i and my family would also end up in a goddam concentration camp. we europeans have the power to stand up for fundamental rights against china’s digital dictatorship. let’s start with targeted economic sanctions. mr borrell: act!
